Does it make me feel better? Um... I don't know. Maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess in the moment I'm not really thinking about feeling better or not.

I told my sister she should just let us pick our own styles in the same color family, but she is obsessed with everyone matching perfectly for the photos. It's exhausting dealing with it all. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no, I haven't tracked if anxiety makes me eat differently. I just honestly don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or frustrated with work or other things?\n\nPatient: Does it make me feel better? Um... I don't know. Maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess in the moment I'm not really thinking about feeling better or not. \n\nBut honestly, you know what would actually make me feel better? If my sister just eloped. Seriously, the venue she picked is this converted barn out in the country. Which sounds cute, right? Rustic chic or whatever. But it's in the middle of literally nowhere, and the cell service is completely nonexistent. I was out there with her last weekend to look at the lighting setup because she wants this very specific fairy-light canopy, and my phone was just a brick the entire time. Which is an absolute nightmare when you're a freelancer waiting on client feedback. I had this one client who needed a rush revision on a digital brochure by 5 PM, and I couldn't even get the PDF to attach to my email. I had to drive three miles down a literal dirt road just to get one bar of signal so it would send. Wait, coping with stress? I mean... I feel like you've kind of asked me this a few times now in different ways. And honestly, my answer is still just... I don't know. I really couldn't tell you if I'm doing it to cope. I'm just eating. 

But speaking of coping, I really don't know how my mom is coping with all this wedding stuff either. She offered to pay for the floral arrangements, which was super nice of her, but my sister took that as a blank check to hire this boutique florist who only works with locally sourced, organic, pesticide-free blooms. Which, fine, whatever, save the bees, but the quote they gave my mom was astronomical. Like, you could buy a decent used car for what these flowers cost. And half of them are basically weeds! My mom called me yesterday practically in tears because she doesn't want to tell my sister she can't afford it, but she also can't pull that kind of money out of thin air. I told her to just be honest, but my mom hates conflict. She'll probably just put it on a credit card and stress about it for the next five years. It's so ridiculous. 

But yeah, the eating and coping thing... I just don't know. I've never really analyzed it like that.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, it's definitely only when I'm alone. Like, if I'm out with friends or having lunch with coworkers, I'm super careful to just eat a normal amount, or even less. I'll get a salad or something. But when I'm alone in my condo, especially after midnight, that's when it happens. I even close the blinds and make sure to throw away all the delivery containers right away because I would be absolutely mortified if anyone saw how much food there was. 

Which, honestly, getting rid of trash in my building is its own complete nightmare right now. The condo association decided to switch waste management companies last month to save a few bucks, and it has been a disaster. The new company only picks up the recycling every other week, and they completely got rid of the large item dumpster. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's like eighty, she tried to throw away this old broken microwave, and the super actually knocked on her door and made her drag it back into her apartment! Can you imagine? And the regular trash chutes are always jammed now because people are trying to stuff oversized garbage bags down them instead of taking them to the basement. I had to carry a bag down three flights of stairs yesterday because the elevator was being serviced—again. They just raised our HOA fees by fifteen percent, and for what? Broken elevators and overflowing trash chutes. It's infuriating. 

But yeah, to answer your question, I definitely only eat like that when I'm completely alone. I could never do it in front of anyone else.

week where this happens, sometimes more, and that pattern has been there for several months.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about ten months, since shortly after I closed on the condo and started taking on all the extra freelance work.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 33 / graphic_designer / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S049\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or frustrated with work or other things?\n\nPatient: Does it make me feel better? Um... I don't know. Maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess in the moment I'm not really thinking about feeling better or not. \n\nBut honestly, you know what would actually make me feel better? If my sister just eloped. Seriously, the venue she picked is this converted barn out in the country. Which sounds cute, right? Rustic chic or whatever. But it's in the middle of literally nowhere, and the cell service is completely nonexistent. I was out there with her last weekend to look at the lighting setup because she wants this very specific fairy-light canopy, and my phone was just a brick the entire time. Which is an absolute nightmare when you're a freelancer waiting on client feedback. I had this one client who needed a rush revision on a digital brochure by 5 PM, and I couldn't even get the PDF to attach to my email. I had to drive three miles down a literal dirt road just to get one bar of signal so it would send. My car was completely covered in dust by the time I got back, and Barnaby was in the backseat just sneezing non-stop from it... \n\nAnyway, sorry, what did you ask? If the eating makes me feel better? Yeah, I really don't know. I've just never paid attention to whether it actually helps with the stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is something you do to cope with stress?\n\nPatient: Wait, coping with stress? I mean... I feel like you've kind of asked me this a few times now in different ways. And honestly, my answer is still just... I don't know. I really couldn't tell you if I'm doing it to cope. I'm just eating. \n\nBut speaking of coping, I really don't know how my mom is coping with all this wedding stuff either. She offered to pay for the floral arrangements, which was super nice of her, but my sister took that as a blank check to hire this boutique florist who only works with locally sourced, organic, pesticide-free blooms. Which, fine, whatever, save the bees, but the quote they gave my mom was astronomical. Like, you could buy a decent used car for what these flowers cost. And half of them are basically weeds! My mom called me yesterday practically in tears because she doesn't want to tell my sister she can't afford it, but she also can't pull that kind of money out of thin air. I told her to just be honest, but my mom hates conflict. She'll probably just put it on a credit card and stress about it for the next five years. It's so ridiculous. \n\nBut yeah, the eating and coping thing... I just don't know. I've never really analyzed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this much when you're alone, or is it just when you're working late?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely only when I'm alone. Like, if I'm out with friends or having lunch with coworkers, I'm super careful to just eat a normal amount, or even less. I'll get a salad or something. But when I'm alone in my condo, especially after midnight, that's when it happens. I even close the blinds and make sure to throw away all the delivery containers right away because I would be absolutely mortified if anyone saw how much food there was. \n\nWhich, honestly, getting rid of trash in my building is its own complete nightmare right now. The condo association decided to switch waste management companies last month to save a few bucks, and it has been a disaster. The new company only picks up the recycling every other week, and they completely got rid of the large item dumpster. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's like eighty, she tried to throw away this old broken microwave, and the super actually knocked on her door and made her drag it back into her apartment! Can you imagine? And the regular trash chutes are always jammed now because people are trying to stuff oversized garbage bags down them instead of taking them to the basement. I had to carry a bag down three flights of stairs yesterday because the elevator was being serviced—again. They just raised our HOA fees by fifteen percent, and for what? Broken elevators and overflowing trash chutes. It's infuriating. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I definitely only eat like that when I'm completely alone. I could never do it in front of anyone else.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think you would eat this much if you weren't feeling stressed or frustrated?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I mean, if I wasn't stressed? I really couldn't say. I just... I don't know if the stress has anything to do with it, honestly. I've never really tried to connect the two. 

But speaking of stress, you know what's really stressing me out right now? My car's registration is due, and the DMV website is completely broken. Like, I spent an hour yesterday just trying to log in. Every time I hit submit, it just gave me this weird error code. I even tried calling them, which, you know, is basically a joke. I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to this awful elevator music that sounded like it was recorded underwater. And then, when a real person finally answered, we got disconnected after like two seconds! I swear, it's like they design the system specifically to make you lose your mind. Now I have to actually go down there in person, which means taking time off work, and the line is always wrapped around the building by 8 AM. It's just ridiculous. 

But yeah, about the eating... I really have no idea if I'd still do it if I wasn't frustrated with things. I just don't know.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Does it help? Um... I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really thought about whether it actually makes me feel better or if it's just something I do. I don't know if "helps" is the right word. 

But speaking of frustration, oh my god, you would not believe the client I'm dealing with right now at work. I'm a graphic designer, right? And this woman hired me to redesign her bakery's logo. She explicitly told me she wanted something "minimalist and modern." So I spent three days drafting these really clean, beautiful concepts with this gorgeous sans-serif typography. I send them over, and she emails me back saying, "Where are the cartoon cupcakes? I want a giant pink cupcake with googly eyes." Googly eyes! On a modern logo! I tried to explain to her that it totally clashes with the brand identity we discussed, but she just wouldn't listen. And then she had the nerve to ask if I could do it for half the price since "adding a cupcake is easy." I swear, people think graphic design is just clicking a magic button on Photoshop. It's so insulting to my profession. I've been doing this since I got my degree and it never ends with these people. 

So yeah... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, if eating helps the stress. I mean, I guess I don't really know. I just haven't noticed if it actually fixes anything.

myself keep clicking back to the food or opening another package, thinking I should stop, but it feels like the decision has already slipped out of my hands.\n- key: S051\n  name: Rapid_Eating\n  description: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n  manifestation: I eat so fast at my desk that I barely notice the taste, just moving from container to container while Illustrator files are open and then realizing I’ve swallowed most of it in a few minutes.\n- key: S052\n  name: Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full\n  description: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n  manifestation: By the end I’m often so full that my stomach feels tight against my waistband and I have to sit very still on the couch before I can even clean up the takeout bags.\n- key: S053\n  name: Eating_Without_Hunger\n  description: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n  manifestation: A lot of the time it starts after I already had a normal dinner, when I’m not physically hungry at all but I’m still wandering into the kitchen or ordering delivery while I’m trying to finish a project.\n- key: S054\n  name: Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment\n  description: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n  manifestation: I’m careful to eat lightly if I’m with coworkers or friends, but when I’m alone in my condo after midnight I’ll close the blinds and get rid of the delivery containers quickly because I’d be mortified if anyone saw how much there was.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: The binge eating occurs, on average, at least once a week for 3 months\n  manifestation: When I look back at my delivery receipts and calendar, there’s usually at least one night every week where this happens, sometimes more, and that pattern has been there for several months.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about ten months, since shortly after I closed on the condo and started taking on all the extra freelance work.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 33 / graphic_designer / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S049\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this much if you weren't feeling stressed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I wasn't stressed? I really couldn't say. I just... I don't know if the stress has anything to do with it, honestly. I've never really tried to connect the two. \n\nBut speaking of stress, you know what's really stressing me out right now? My car's registration is due, and the DMV website is completely broken. Like, I spent an hour yesterday just trying to log in. Every time I hit submit, it just gave me this weird error code. I even tried calling them, which, you know, is basically a joke. I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to this awful elevator music that sounded like it was recorded underwater. And then, when a real person finally answered, we got disconnected after like two seconds! I swear, it's like they design the system specifically to make you lose your mind. He's only been taking lessons for like six months. And the worst part is, my aunt is totally enabling him. She thinks it's hilarious, but I told her, when his wife finds out—and she will find out—it is going to be World War III. They already fight all the time about money. I definitely hide it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you deal with your stress and frustration?\n\nPatient: Does it help? Um... I'm not really sure, to be honest. I send them over, and she emails me back saying, \"Where are the cartoon cupcakes? I want a giant pink cupcake with googly eyes.\" Googly eyes! On a modern logo! I tried to explain to her that it totally clashes with the brand identity we discussed, but she just wouldn't listen. If I wasn't working late? I mean, I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really had a period where I wasn't stressed out to compare it to, so I don't know what I would do. 

But speaking of working late, my friend Sarah just got a job at this new tech startup, and they do a four-day work week. Can you imagine? She has every single Friday off. And not only that, but they have this strict rule where they actually lock everyone out of the company Slack and email servers at 5 PM on Thursday. Like, you physically cannot log in to work even if you wanted to. She was telling me about it this weekend when we went to this farmer's market—which, by the way, was totally overpriced. They were charging like eight dollars for a single heirloom tomato. Who pays eight dollars for a tomato? 

Anyway, Sarah says her productivity has actually gone up since she started there because she's so well-rested. I pitched the idea of just leaving at 5 PM to my boss, and he basically laughed in my face. He's one of those "hustle culture" guys who thinks if you aren't grinding 60 hours a week, you're lazy. It's so toxic. 

But yeah, wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, if I'd still eat like that without the stress. I honestly couldn't tell you. It's just hard to say since I'm pretty much always stressed.

I really couldn't say. I just... I don't know if the stress has anything to do with it, honestly. Like, I spent an hour yesterday just trying to log in. Every time I hit submit, it just gave me this weird error code. I even tried calling them, which, you know, is basically a joke. He told her he was saving up for a down payment on a new house, but instead, he blew a huge chunk of it on this guitar that he can't even play that well! Comfort myself? Um... I don't know, honestly. I haven't really paid attention to whether it's about comfort or just... I don't know, just something that happens when I'm by myself.

She picked this chiffon fabric that literally wrinkles if you even look at it wrong. My cousin Sarah is refusing to wear it because she says it washes her out, which, honestly, she's not wrong. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Make myself feel better? Um... I don't really know. I've never really thought about it like that, like if I'm doing it specifically because I'm upset. 

But you know what did upset me recently? I took my car in for just a regular oil change, right? And the mechanic comes out with this clipboard looking all serious, telling me I need new brake pads, new rotors, a new cabin air filter, and that my transmission fluid is practically sludge. He quoted me like twelve hundred dollars! I was so mad because I just had it inspected six months ago and everything was fine. I actually called my dad from the waiting room because he used to rebuild old muscle cars in the nineties, and he told me to ask the guy to physically show me the brake pads. When I asked him to do that, suddenly he was like, "Well, they're in the yellow zone, you could probably wait until your next service." It's just so frustrating how they try to upsell you the second you walk in the door. I ended up just getting the oil change and leaving, but I was fuming the whole drive home. 

Wait, what were we just talking about? Oh, eating when I'm upset. Yeah, I don't really know if I do that. I just haven't really made that connection.

I've never really analyzed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this much when you're alone, or is it just when you're working late?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely only when I'm alone. Like, if I'm out with friends or having lunch with coworkers, I'm super careful to just eat a normal amount, or even less. I'll get a salad or something. But when I'm alone in my condo, especially after midnight, that's when it happens. I even close the blinds and make sure to throw away all the delivery containers right away because I would be absolutely mortified if anyone saw how much food there was. \n\nWhich, honestly, getting rid of trash in my building is its own complete nightmare right now. The condo association decided to switch waste management companies last month to save a few bucks, and it has been a disaster. The new company only picks up the recycling every other week, and they completely got rid of the large item dumpster. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's like eighty, she tried to throw away this old broken microwave, and the super actually knocked on her door and made her drag it back into her apartment! Can you imagine? And the regular trash chutes are always jammed now because people are trying to stuff oversized garbage bags down them instead of taking them to the basement. I had to carry a bag down three flights of stairs yesterday because the elevator was being serviced—again. They just raised our HOA fees by fifteen percent, and for what? Broken elevators and overflowing trash chutes. It's infuriating. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I definitely only eat like that when I'm completely alone. I could never do it in front of anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this much if you weren't feeling stressed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I wasn't stressed? I really couldn't say. I just... I don't know if the stress has anything to do with it, honestly. I've never really tried to connect the two. \n\nBut speaking of stress, you know what's really stressing me out right now? My car's registration is due, and the DMV website is completely broken. Like, I spent an hour yesterday just trying to log in. Every time I hit submit, it just gave me this weird error code. I even tried calling them, which, you know, is basically a joke. I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to this awful elevator music that sounded like it was recorded underwater. And then, when a real person finally answered, we got disconnected after like two seconds! I swear, it's like they design the system specifically to make you lose your mind. Now I have to actually go down there in person, which means taking time off work, and the line is always wrapped around the building by 8 AM. It's just ridiculous. \n\nBut yeah, about the eating... I really have no idea if I'd still do it if I wasn't frustrated with things. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide the fact that you're eating this much from others?\n\nPatient: Wait, hide the fact that I'm eating? I mean, I thought I just answered that, didn't I? I told you about how I only do it when I'm alone and how I throw away all the delivery containers right away. I'm pretty sure I already went over all that. \n\nBut speaking of hiding things, you wouldn't believe what my cousin Mark is trying to hide from his wife right now. He bought this ridiculously expensive vintage electric guitar—like, we're talking thousands of dollars—and he's keeping it at my aunt's house because he doesn't want his wife to find out. He told her he was saving up for a down payment on a new house, but instead, he blew a huge chunk of it on this guitar that he can't even play that well! He's only been taking lessons for like six months. And the worst part is, my aunt is totally enabling him. She thinks it's hilarious, but I told her, when his wife finds out—and she will find out—it is going to be World War III. They already fight all the time about money. I honestly don't know how they're still married. It's just so deceitful, you know? \n\nBut yeah, about hiding the food... I really feel like we already talked about that. I definitely hide it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you deal with your stress and frustration?\n\nPatient: Does it help? Um... I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really thought about whether it actually makes me feel better or if it's just something I do. I don't know if \"helps\" is the right word. \n\nBut speaking of frustration, oh my god, you would not believe the client I'm dealing with right now at work. I'm a graphic designer, right? And this woman hired me to redesign her bakery's logo. She explicitly told me she wanted something \"minimalist and modern.\" So I spent three days drafting these really clean, beautiful concepts with this gorgeous sans-serif typography. I send them over, and she emails me back saying, \"Where are the cartoon cupcakes? I want a giant pink cupcake with googly eyes.\" Googly eyes! On a modern logo! I tried to explain to her that it totally clashes with the brand identity we discussed, but she just wouldn't listen. And then she had the nerve to ask if I could do it for half the price since \"adding a cupcake is easy.\" I swear, people think graphic design is just clicking a magic button on Photoshop. It's so insulting to my profession. I've been doing this since I got my degree and it never ends with these people. \n\nSo yeah... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, if eating helps the stress. I mean, I guess I don't really know. I just haven't noticed if it actually fixes anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to deal with your feelings?\n\nPatient: Deal with my feelings? I mean... I don't really know. I've never really stopped to analyze if it's an emotional thing or what. I just haven't really thought about it like that. \n\nBut speaking of feelings, my sister is having a lot of feelings lately about her wedding. She's getting married in October, and she decided to make all the bridesmaids' dresses herself. Which, okay, ambitious, right? Especially since she barely knows how to sew! She bought this vintage sewing machine from a thrift store, and it keeps jamming. Yesterday, she called me literally sobbing because she accidentally sewed the sleeve of the maid of honor's dress to the hem. I told her to just buy them off the rack—there are so many good options online now—but she's so stubborn. She wants that \"handmade rustic aesthetic\" for the photos. It's driving the whole bridal party crazy. My mom is actually threatening to secretly hire a tailor to fix everything behind her back so we don't all walk down the aisle looking like we're wearing potato sacks. It's just a total mess. \n\nBut anyway... what did you ask? Oh, right, if I eat because of my feelings. Yeah, I just really couldn't say. I haven't noticed if that's why I do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this much if you weren't working late and dealing with stressful situations?\n\nPatient: If I wasn't working late? I mean, I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really had a period where I wasn't stressed out to compare it to, so I don't know what I would do. \n\nBut speaking of working late, my friend Sarah just got a job at this new tech startup, and they do a four-day work week. Can you imagine? She has every single Friday off. And not only that, but they have this strict rule where they actually lock everyone out of the company Slack and email servers at 5 PM on Thursday. Like, you physically cannot log in to work even if you wanted to. She was telling me about it this weekend when we went to this farmer's market—which, by the way, was totally overpriced. They were charging like eight dollars for a single heirloom tomato. Who pays eight dollars for a tomato? \n\nAnyway, Sarah says her productivity has actually gone up since she started there because she's so well-rested. I pitched the idea of just leaving at 5 PM to my boss, and he basically laughed in my face. He's one of those \"hustle culture\" guys who thinks if you aren't grinding 60 hours a week, you're lazy. It's so toxic. \n\nBut yeah, wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, if I'd still eat like that without the stress. I honestly couldn't tell you. It's just hard to say since I'm pretty much always stressed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're alone as a way to comfort yourself?\n\nPatient: Comfort myself? Um... I don't know, honestly. I haven't really paid attention to whether it's about comfort or just... I don't know, just something that happens when I'm by myself. \n\nBut speaking of being alone, I actually just started trying to grow my own houseplants because my apartment felt so empty. I bought this fiddle leaf fig, which everyone on Instagram makes look so easy, right? They just stick it in a corner and it looks like a magazine shoot. Well, let me tell you, those things are so incredibly dramatic. If you move it two inches to the left, it drops three leaves. If you look at it wrong, it turns brown. I bought this special moisture meter and this expensive organic fertilizer, and I swear it's still dying. I was at the garden center for like two hours on Sunday just talking to this guy who works there—I think his name was Gary—about soil drainage. Stressed? I mean, I guess I've never really sat down and thought about whether eating is a coping mechanism for stress. I don't really know if they're linked like that for me. 

But talking about stress, you wouldn't believe the week I've had with my upstairs neighbors. They just got this puppy—a husky mix, I think—and they leave it alone all day while they're at work. It literally howls for eight hours straight. I tried to be cool about it, you know? The walls in this building are paper thin. I can hear when he drops his keys, let alone a dog singing the song of its people all afternoon. I had to buy these expensive noise-canceling headphones just to get any work done, but they give me a headache if I wear them for more than an hour. 

Anyway... sorry, I get so worked up about it. What did you ask? Oh, right, eating to cope with stress. Honestly, I'm just not sure. I haven't really paid attention to whether stress is what triggers it.

I haven't really noticed if it's the stress specifically. I'm just sitting there staring at my screen. But honestly, it's hard not to be stressed when people don't understand basic design principles. Like, I had this other client last year who wanted me to design a logo for a meditation app, and they insisted on using fire engine red. Red! For a calming meditation space! I tried to explain how color psychology works, but they just wouldn't listen to me. I actually ended up having to drop them because it was going to completely ruin my portfolio. Oh, and my dog, Barnaby, he always gets so needy when I'm working late on stuff like that, too. He'll just sit under my desk and whine and paw at my leg while I'm trying to focus on my monitor, which doesn't help anything. \n\nUm... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right. I don't know, maybe? I haven't really tracked it like that. I just know I eat a lot when I'm working late. Like, I'll be sitting there, and suddenly I've eaten everything. But honestly, it's hard not to be anxious lately. My sister is planning her wedding, and she wants me to do all the invitations, the menus, the seating charts... everything. And she's being a total bridezilla about it. She wants this specific shade of blush pink that is just impossible to print consistently. I swear, I've gone through three different local printers, and none of them can get it right. The last guy I talked to at the print shop was so rude about it, too. He acted like I was being unreasonable, but I'm just trying to make my sister happy, you know? And Barnaby—my dog—he chewed up one of the test prints yesterday, which just added to the chaos. \n\nWait, sorry, what was the question again? About being upset? Yeah, I'm not really sure if that's what triggers the eating. I just haven't noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating changes when you're feeling anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? I really don't know, I'm sorry. Like I said, I haven't really noticed if being upset or anxious changes anything. I just know the eating happens when I'm up late working. \n\nAnd speaking of being up late, my sister actually called me at 11:30 last night completely freaking out about the bridesmaid dresses. She picked this chiffon fabric that literally wrinkles if you even look at it wrong. My cousin Sarah is refusing to wear it because she says it washes her out, which, honestly, she's not wrong. I just honestly don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or frustrated with work or other things?\n\nPatient: Does it make me feel better? Um... I don't know. Maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. If the eating makes me feel better? Yeah, I really don't know. I've just never paid attention to whether it actually helps with the stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is something you do to cope with stress?\n\nPatient: Wait, coping with stress? I mean... I feel like you've kind of asked me this a few times now in different ways. And honestly, my answer is still just... I don't know. I really couldn't tell you if I'm doing it to cope. I'm just eating. \n\nBut speaking of coping, I really don't know how my mom is coping with all this wedding stuff either. She offered to pay for the floral arrangements, which was super nice of her, but my sister took that as a blank check to hire this boutique florist who only works with locally sourced, organic, pesticide-free blooms. Which, fine, whatever, save the bees, but the quote they gave my mom was astronomical. Like, you could buy a decent used car for what these flowers cost. And half of them are basically weeds! My mom called me yesterday practically in tears because she doesn't want to tell my sister she can't afford it, but she also can't pull that kind of money out of thin air. I told her to just be honest, but my mom hates conflict. She'll probably just put it on a credit card and stress about it for the next five years. It's so ridiculous. \n\nBut yeah, the eating and coping thing... I just don't know. I've never really analyzed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this much when you're alone, or is it just when you're working late?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely only when I'm alone. Like, if I'm out with friends or having lunch with coworkers, I'm super careful to just eat a normal amount, or even less. I'll get a salad or something. But when I'm alone in my condo, especially after midnight, that's when it happens. I even close the blinds and make sure to throw away all the delivery containers right away because I would be absolutely mortified if anyone saw how much food there was. \n\nWhich, honestly, getting rid of trash in my building is its own complete nightmare right now. The condo association decided to switch waste management companies last month to save a few bucks, and it has been a disaster. The new company only picks up the recycling every other week, and they completely got rid of the large item dumpster. Can you imagine? And the regular trash chutes are always jammed now because people are trying to stuff oversized garbage bags down them instead of taking them to the basement. Broken elevators and overflowing trash chutes. It's infuriating. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I definitely only eat like that when I'm completely alone. I could never do it in front of anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this much if you weren't feeling stressed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I wasn't stressed? I really couldn't say. I just... I don't know if the stress has anything to do with it, honestly. I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to this awful elevator music that sounded like it was recorded underwater. I really have no idea if I'd still do it if I wasn't frustrated with things. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to hide the fact that you're eating this much from others?\n\nPatient: Wait, hide the fact that I'm eating? He told her he was saving up for a down payment on a new house, but instead, he blew a huge chunk of it on this guitar that he can't even play that well! He's only been taking lessons for like six months. And the worst part is, my aunt is totally enabling him. I definitely hide it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you deal with your stress and frustration?\n\nPatient: Does it help? Um... I'm not really sure, to be honest. And then she had the nerve to ask if I could do it for half the price since \"adding a cupcake is easy.\" I swear, people think graphic design is just clicking a magic button on Photoshop. I haven't really thought about whether it actually makes me feel better or if it's just something I do. I don't know if \"helps\" is the right word. \n\nBut speaking of frustration, oh my god, you would not believe the client I'm dealing with right now at work. I'm a graphic designer, right? She explicitly told me she wanted something \"minimalist and modern.\" So I spent three days drafting these really clean, beautiful concepts with this gorgeous sans-serif typography. I send them over, and she emails me back saying, \"Where are the cartoon cupcakes? I want a giant pink cupcake with googly eyes.\" Googly eyes! On a modern logo! I tried to explain to her that it totally clashes with the brand identity we discussed, but she just wouldn't listen. And then she had the nerve to ask if I could do it for half the price since \"adding a cupcake is easy.\" I swear, people think graphic design is just clicking a magic button on Photoshop. It's so insulting to my profession. I've been doing this since I got my degree and it never ends with these people. \n\nSo yeah... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, if eating helps the stress. I mean, I guess I don't really know. I just haven't noticed if it actually fixes anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to deal with your feelings?\n\nPatient: Deal with my feelings? I mean... I don't really know. I've never really stopped to analyze if it's an emotional thing or what. I just haven't really thought about it like that. \n\nBut speaking of feelings, my sister is having a lot of feelings lately about her wedding. She's getting married in October, and she decided to make all the bridesmaids' dresses herself. Which, okay, ambitious, right? Especially since she barely knows how to sew! She bought this vintage sewing machine from a thrift store, and it keeps jamming. It's driving the whole bridal party crazy. Like, you physically cannot log in to work even if you wanted to. She was telling me about it this weekend when we went to this farmer's market—which, by the way, was totally overpriced. They were charging like eight dollars for a single heirloom tomato. Who pays eight dollars for a tomato? \n\nAnyway, Sarah says her productivity has actually gone up since she started there because she's so well-rested. It's so toxic. \n\nBut yeah, wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, if I'd still eat like that without the stress. I honestly couldn't tell you. Well, let me tell you, those things are so incredibly dramatic. If you move it two inches to the left, it drops three leaves. If you look at it wrong, it turns brown. I bought this special moisture meter and this expensive organic fertilizer, and I swear it's still dying. I was at the garden center for like two hours on Sunday just talking to this guy who works there—I think his name was Gary—about soil drainage. He was telling me about how people overwater them because they think they're being nice, but they're actually drowning the roots. It's crazy how much science goes into just keeping a plant alive in your living room. \n\nSo... wait, what was the question? Oh, right, about eating for comfort when I'm alone. Yeah, I'm really not sure about that. I just honestly don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or frustrated with work or other things?\n\nPatient: Does it make me feel better? Um... I don't know. Maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. I'm just eating. \n\nBut speaking of coping, I really don't know how my mom is coping with all this wedding stuff either. It's so ridiculous. \n\nBut yeah, the eating and coping thing... I just don't know. I've never really analyzed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this much when you're alone, or is it just when you're working late?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely only when I'm alone. But when I'm alone in my condo, especially after midnight, that's when it happens. The new company only picks up the recycling every other week, and they completely got rid of the large item dumpster. Broken elevators and overflowing trash chutes. It's infuriating. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I definitely only eat like that when I'm completely alone. I could never do it in front of anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this much if you weren't feeling stressed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I wasn't stressed? I really couldn't say. I just... I don't know if the stress has anything to do with it, honestly. Like, I spent an hour yesterday just trying to log in. Every time I hit submit, it just gave me this weird error code. I even tried calling them, which, you know, is basically a joke. I just haven't noticed if it actually fixes anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to deal with your feelings?\n\nPatient: Deal with my feelings? I mean... I don't really know. I just haven't really thought about it like that. \n\nBut speaking of feelings, my sister is having a lot of feelings lately about her wedding. I told her to just buy them off the rack—there are so many good options online now—but she's so stubborn. She wants that \"handmade rustic aesthetic\" for the photos. It's driving the whole bridal party crazy. My mom is actually threatening to secretly hire a tailor to fix everything behind her back so we don't all walk down the aisle looking like we're wearing potato sacks. It's just a total mess. \n\nBut anyway... what did you ask? Oh, right, if I eat because of my feelings. Yeah, I just really couldn't say. I haven't noticed if that's why I do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still eat this much if you weren't working late and dealing with stressful situations?\n\nPatient: If I wasn't working late? I mean, I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really had a period where I wasn't stressed out to compare it to, so I don't know what I would do. \n\nBut speaking of working late, my friend Sarah just got a job at this new tech startup, and they do a four-day work week. Can you imagine? She has every single Friday off. And not only that, but they have this strict rule where they actually lock everyone out of the company Slack and email servers at 5 PM on Thursday. Like, you physically cannot log in to work even if you wanted to. She was telling me about it this weekend when we went to this farmer's market—which, by the way, was totally overpriced. I bought this special moisture meter and this expensive organic fertilizer, and I swear it's still dying. I was at the garden center for like two hours on Sunday just talking to this guy who works there—I think his name was Gary—about soil drainage. Sad or upset? I mean... maybe? I really haven't kept track of it like that. I don't know if I can say for sure that feeling sad is what makes me eat. 

Though honestly, I was pretty upset yesterday. I had this huge deadline for a branding package for a new client—this boutique coffee shop downtown. I spent three weeks perfecting their logo, getting the typography exactly how they described they wanted it in the brief. I sent over the final proofs, and the owner emails me back saying, "Actually, we decided to go in a more minimalist direction, can you just use Helvetica and make it black and white?" Helvetica! After I custom-drew this beautiful retro-inspired font for them! I was so frustrated I almost cried. It's like people think graphic design is just pushing a button on a computer. And the worst part is, they still expect it by Friday, which means I have to scrap weeks of work and start over, but they don't want to pay for the extra revisions because they claim it's "simpler" now. 

Oh, gosh, sorry. I'm venting again. You asked about eating when I'm sad, right? Yeah, I just... I'm not sure. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern with that.

I definitely hide it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you deal with your stress and frustration?\n\nPatient: Does it help? Um... I'm not really sure, to be honest. She bought this vintage sewing machine from a thrift store, and it keeps jamming. Yesterday, she called me literally sobbing because she accidentally sewed the sleeve of the maid of honor's dress to the hem. I told her to just buy them off the rack—there are so many good options online now—but she's so stubborn. She wants that \"handmade rustic aesthetic\" for the photos. It's driving the whole bridal party crazy. It's just a total mess. \n\nBut anyway... what did you ask? Oh, right, if I eat because of my feelings. Yeah, I just really couldn't say. Who pays eight dollars for a tomato? \n\nAnyway, Sarah says her productivity has actually gone up since she started there because she's so well-rested. Overwhelmed? I... I don't know. I guess I've never really stopped to think if being overwhelmed is what sets it off. It's hard to say if it actually makes me feel better.

It's so ridiculous. \n\nBut yeah, the eating and coping thing... I just don't know. I've never really analyzed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this much when you're alone, or is it just when you're working late?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely only when I'm alone. Like, if I'm out with friends or having lunch with coworkers, I'm super careful to just eat a normal amount, or even less. I'll get a salad or something. But when I'm alone in my condo, especially after midnight, that's when it happens. The new company only picks up the recycling every other week, and they completely got rid of the large item dumpster. Can you imagine? And the regular trash chutes are always jammed now because people are trying to stuff oversized garbage bags down them instead of taking them to the basement. I had to carry a bag down three flights of stairs yesterday because the elevator was being serviced—again. They just raised our HOA fees by fifteen percent, and for what? Broken elevators and overflowing trash chutes. It's infuriating. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I definitely only eat like that when I'm completely alone. I could never do it in front of anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat this much if you weren't feeling stressed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I mean, if I wasn't stressed? Stressed? Um, I guess I just don't really know. I've never really sat down and tracked if I'm eating specifically because of stress, you know? It's just hard to pinpoint a reason like that.
